=== README (first 120 lines) ===
# opencascade-binding-generator

Automatically generates Rust \`extern "C"\` FFI bindings for the [OpenCASCADE](https://dev.opencascade.org/) C++ CAD kernel.

Given a set of OCCT header files, the generator produces a complete Rust FFI layer: type declarations, method bindings, constructor wrappers, Handle smart pointer support, collection iterators, inheritance upcasts, and per-module re-exports with ergonomic short names.

## Quick Start

\`\`\`bash
# Regenerate all bindings (from repo root)
./scripts/regenerate-bindings.sh
\`\`\`

This parses OCCT headers configured in \`bindings.toml\` (expanding with automatic dependency resolution), and writes generated code to \`crates/opencascade-sys/generated/\`.

## CLI Usage

\`\`\`bash
# Standard regeneration (from repo root):
./scripts/regenerate-bindings.sh

# Manual invocation with TOML config:
cargo run -p opencascade-binding-generator -- \
    --config crates/opencascade-sys/bindings.toml \
    -I target/OCCT/include \
    -o crates/opencascade-sys/generated

# Or with explicit header arguments (legacy):
cargo run -p opencascade-binding-generator -- \
    -I target/OCCT/include \
    -o crates/opencascade-sys/generated \
    target/OCCT/include/gp_Pnt.hxx target/OCCT/include/TopoDS_Shape.hxx ...
\`\`\`

**Flags:**
- \`--config <file>\` — TOML configuration file specifying headers (recommended)
- \`--resolve-deps\` — Auto-include header dependencies (default: true)
- \`--dump-symbols\` — Dump symbol table for debugging
- \`--dry-run\` — Parse without generating
- \`-v, --verbose\` — Verbose output
- \`--module <name>\` — Filter to a specific module

## Generated Public API

Users interact with \`opencascade-sys\` through per-module re-exports. Each OCCT module (gp, TopoDS, BRepPrimAPI, etc.) becomes a Rust module with short type names and idiomatic method signatures.

### Geometry Primitives

Simple value types like points, vectors, and directions get constructors, accessors, and transformation methods. All methods go through extern "C" C++ wrappers. Methods that return class types by value return \`OwnedPtr<T>\`.

\`\`\`rust
use opencascade_sys::gp::{Pnt, Vec, Dir, Ax1, Ax2, Trsf};

// Constructors -- overloads distinguished by parameter type suffix
let origin = Pnt::new();                          // default constructor
let p = Pnt::new_real3(1.0, 2.0, 3.0);           // from coordinates
let p2 = Pnt::new_xyz(&xyz);                      // from XYZ

// Direct accessors
let x: f64 = p.x();
let y: f64 = p.y();
p.set_x(10.0);

// Transformations return OwnedPtr (by-value return needs C++ wrapper)
let mirrored: OwnedPtr<Pnt> = p.mirrored_pnt(&origin);
let rotated = p.rotated(&axis, std::f64::consts::FRAC_PI_2);
let transformed = p.transformed(&trsf);

// Clone into a new OwnedPtr
let p_copy = p.clone();

\`\`\`

### Topology Types and Inheritance

Topological shapes form an inheritance hierarchy: \`Shape\` is the base, with subtypes \`Vertex\`, \`Edge\`, \`Wire\`, \`Face\`, \`Shell\`, \`Solid\`, \`CompSolid\`, and \`Compound\`. Extern "C" FFI treats these as unrelated opaque types, so the generator produces explicit upcast methods (both const and mutable) and downcast free functions.

\`\`\`rust
use opencascade_sys::topo_ds::{Shape, Edge, Face, Solid, Wire};

// Upcasting -- every subtype can upcast to its ancestors
let edge = Edge::new();
let shape_ref: &Shape = edge.as_shape();                    // const upcast
let shape_mut: &mut Shape = edge.as_shape_mut();            // mutable upcast

// Downcasting -- free functions in the topo_ds module
// (wraps OCCT's TopoDS::Edge(), TopoDS::Face(), etc.)
use opencascade_sys::topo_ds;
let edge: OwnedPtr<Edge> = topo_ds::edge(&some_shape);       // const
let face: OwnedPtr<Face> = topo_ds::face_mut(&mut shape);    // mutable

// Copy semantics (TopoDS shapes are reference-counted internally)
let shape_copy = shape.clone();

\`\`\`

### Primitive Shape Construction (Builder Pattern)

OCCT builder classes follow a pattern: construct with parameters, then extract the result by upcasting to \`MakeShape\` and calling \`shape()\`.

\`\`\`rust
use opencascade_sys::gp::{Pnt, Ax2};
use opencascade_sys::b_rep_prim_api::{MakeBox, MakeSphere, MakeCylinder};

// Overloaded constructors use parameter type suffixes
let mut make_box = MakeBox::new_real3(10.0, 20.0, 30.0);    // dx, dy, dz
let mut make_box = MakeBox::new_pnt_real3(&p, 10.0, 20.0, 30.0); // corner + size
let mut make_box = MakeBox::new_pnt2(&p1, &p2);             // two corners
let mut make_box = MakeBox::new_ax2_real3(&axes, 10.0, 20.0, 30.0); // local coords

// Extract the result via mutable upcast to MakeShape
let make_shape = make_box.as_b_rep_builder_api_make_shape_mut();
let result: &TopoDS_Shape = make_shape.shape();
\`\`\`

### Boolean Operations

\`\`\`rust
use opencascade_sys::b_rep_algo_api::{Fuse, Cut, Common};
use opencascade_sys::message::ProgressRange;
```
</details>

807	        match self {
808	            Type::Void => "void".to_string(),
809	            Type::Bool => "bool".to_string(),
810	            Type::I32 => "int32_t".to_string(),
811	            Type::U32 => "uint32_t".to_string(),
812	            Type::U16 => "uint16_t".to_string(),

814	            Type::I64 => "int64_t".to_string(),

817	            Type::ULong => "unsigned long".to_string(),
818	            Type::Usize => "size_t".to_string(),

824	            // Use postfix const ("T const&") rather than prefix ("const T&") so that
825	            // when the inner type is itself a pointer (e.g., ConstRef(MutPtr(Class("X")))),
826	            // the const correctly qualifies the pointer level, not the pointee:
827	            //   ConstRef(MutPtr(X)) → "X* const&" (correct: const-ref to pointer-to-X)
828	            //   vs. "const X*&" (wrong: ref to pointer-to-const-X)
829	            // For simple types, "T const&" and "const T&" are equivalent in C/C++.
830	            Type::ConstRef(inner) => format!("{} const&", inner.to_cpp_string()),

842	    /// Check if this type is an unresolved template instantiation that can't be
843	    /// represented in Rust FFI. Only catches template types with \`<>\`.
844	    /// Non-underscore class names (e.g., \`LDOMString\`) are NOT caught here —
845	    /// they are handled by \`type_uses_unknown_class()\` in the binding layer
846	    /// which checks against the symbol table.
847	    fn is_unresolved_template_type(&self) -> bool {
848	        match self {
849	            Type::Class(name) => {
850	                // Template types with angle brackets are not representable
851	                name.contains('<') || name.contains('>')
852	            }
853	            Type::ConstRef(inner) | Type::MutRef(inner) | Type::RValueRef(inner) | Type::ConstPtr(inner) | Type::MutPtr(inner) => {
854	                inner.is_unresolved_template_type()
855	            }
856	            Type::FixedArray(inner, _) => inner.is_unresolved_template_type(),
857	            _ => false,
858	        }

878	            Type::U8 => "u8".to_string(),

201	/// Maps a C++ element type to its Rust FFI representation.
202	/// Primitive types like Standard_Real map to f64, while compound types
203	/// like gp_Pnt2d stay as-is (they exist as opaque structs in the FFI).
204	pub fn rust_element_type(cpp_type: &str) -> &str {
205	
206	    match cpp_type {
207	        "Standard_Real" | "double" => "f64",
208	        "Standard_Integer" | "int" => "i32",
209	        "Standard_Boolean" | "bool" => "bool",
210	        "Standard_ShortReal" | "float" => "f32",
211	        _ => cpp_type,
212	    }
213	}
214	
215	/// Returns true if the element type is a C++ primitive that maps to a Rust native type.
216	pub fn is_primitive_element(cpp_type: &str) -> bool {
217	
218	    matches!(
219	        cpp_type,
220	        "Standard_Real" | "double"
221	            | "Standard_Integer" | "int"
222	            | "Standard_Boolean" | "bool"
223	            | "Standard_ShortReal" | "float"
224	    )
225	}
226	
227	/// Helper to extract module name from a type (e.g., "topo_ds" from "TopoDS_Shape")
228	fn type_to_module(type_name: &str) -> String {
229	    if let Some(underscore_pos) = type_name.find('_') {
230	        crate::module_graph::module_to_rust_name(&type_name[..underscore_pos])
231	    } else {
232	        type_name.to_lowercase()
233	    }
234	}

385	pub fn generate_cpp_collection(info: &CollectionInfo) -> String {
386	    let mut output = String::new();

394	    output.push_str("// ========================\n\n");
395	
396	    // Bridge the mangled canonical identifier to the C++ template spelling so the
397	    // rest of the generation (which uses \`typedef_name\`) refers to the real type.
398	    output.push_str(&format!("typedef {} {typedef_name};\n\n", info.cpp_spelling));
399

402	        "extern \"C\" {typedef_name}* {typedef_name}_new() {{\n"

407	    output.push_str("}\n\n");

414	    output.push_str("}\n\n");

=== handle support region for HArray1/HSequence (440-475) ===
    }

    // Handle-managed collections (HArray1/HSequence) also expose to_handle (wrap an
    // owned collection pointer in an OCCT Handle) and get/get_mut (borrow the pointee).
    if info.kind.is_handle() {
        let handle_ctype = format!("opencascade::handle<{}>", info.cpp_spelling);
        let handle_name = crate::type_mapping::handle_type_name(typedef_name);
        output.push_str(&format!(
            "extern \"C\" {handle_ctype}* {typedef_name}_to_handle({typedef_name}* self_) {{\n"
        ));
        output.push_str(&format!("    return new {handle_ctype}(self_);\n"));
        output.push_str("}\n\n");
        output.push_str(&format!(
            "extern \"C\" const {typedef_name}* {handle_name}_get(const {handle_ctype}* h) {{ return h->get(); }}\n"
        ));
        output.push_str(&format!(
            "extern \"C\" {typedef_name}* {handle_name}_get_mut({handle_ctype}* h) {{ return h->get(); }}\n"
        ));
        // Handle lifetime machinery. The collection owns its handle type, so these are
        // emitted here (the per-toolkit handle loop only covers class-owned handles,
        // and a collection's type is owned by TKernel but used across toolkits).
        output.push_str(&format!(
            "extern \"C\" void {handle_name}_destructor({handle_ctype}* self_) {{ delete self_; }}\n"
        ));
        output.push_str(&format!(
            "extern \"C\" OcctResult<{handle_ctype}*> {handle_name}_clone(const {handle_ctype}* self_) {{ try {{ return {{new {handle_ctype}(*self_), nullptr}}; }} OCCT_CATCH_RETURN }}\n"
        ));
        output.push_str(&format!(
            "extern \"C\" bool {handle_name}_is_null(const {handle_ctype}* self_) {{ return self_->IsNull(); }}\n\n"
        ));
    }

    output
}

```
/// Returns true if the element type is a C++ primitive that maps to a Rust native type.
pub fn is_primitive_element(cpp_type: &str) -> bool {

    matches!(
        cpp_type,
        "Standard_Real" | "double"
            | "Standard_Integer" | "int"
            | "Standard_Boolean" | "bool"
            | "Standard_ShortReal" | "float"
    )
}

/// Maps a deprecated OCCT primitive type alias to its canonical C++ spelling
/// (e.g. `Standard_Boolean` → `bool`), returning the input unchanged for anything
/// that is not a deprecated scalar alias.
///
/// This is applied only to the *C++ type spelling* emitted in wrapper bodies. The
/// FFI symbol names (`typedef_name`, `handle_name`, overload suffix tokens) and the
/// Rust element type are derived separately from the original spelling, so the
/// binding surface is unchanged — this only keeps the generated C++ free of
/// `-Wdeprecated-declarations` warnings. Every alias here is `Standard_DEPRECATED`
/// in `Standard_TypeDef.hxx` and is an exact `typedef` for the canonical type, so
/// substitution is ABI-identical.
pub fn canonical_cpp_primitive(cpp_type: &str) -> &str {
    match cpp_type {
        "Standard_Integer" => "int",
        "Standard_UInteger" => "unsigned int",
        "Standard_Real" => "double",
        "Standard_Boolean" => "bool",
        "Standard_ShortReal" => "float",
        "Standard_Character" => "char",
        "Standard_Byte" => "uint8_t",
        "Standard_Utf8Char" => "char",
        "Standard_Utf8UChar" => "unsigned char",
        "Standard_ExtCharacter" => "char16_t",
        "Standard_Utf16Char" => "char16_t",
        "Standard_Utf32Char" => "char32_t",
        "Standard_WideChar" => "wchar_t",
        "Standard_Size" => "size_t",
        other => other,
    }
}

/// Replace deprecated OCCT primitive aliases with their canonical C++ spellings
/// inside a full template spelling (e.g. `NCollection_HArray1<Standard_Boolean>` →
/// `NCollection_HArray1<bool>`). Matches only whole identifier tokens, so mangled
/// FFI names like `NCollection_HArray1_Standard_Boolean` (where the alias is joined
/// by `_`) are never touched — `\b` treats `_` as a word character.
pub fn canonicalize_cpp_spelling(spelling: &str) -> String {
    use std::sync::OnceLock;
    static RE: OnceLock<regex::Regex> = OnceLock::new();
    let re = RE.get_or_init(|| {
        regex::Regex::new(
            r"\b(Standard_Integer|Standard_UInteger|Standard_Real|Standard_Boolean|Standard_ShortReal|Standard_Character|Standard_Byte|Standard_Utf8Char|Standard_Utf8UChar|Standard_ExtCharacter|Standard_Utf16Char|Standard_Utf32Char|Standard_WideChar|Standard_Size)\b",
        )
        .unwrap()
    });
    re.replace_all(spelling, |caps: &regex::Captures| {
        canonical_cpp_primitive(&caps[1]).to_string()
    })
    .into_owned()
}
```
</details>

pub fn handle_type_name(cpp_name: &str) -> String {
    // Underscore-preserving handle names (e.g. Handle_Geom_Curve,
    // Handle_NCollection_HArray1_gp_Pnt) — this mirrors OCCT's own classic
    // `Handle_X` typedef spelling and keeps the underlying class name readable.
    format!("Handle_{}", cpp_name.replace("::", "_"))
}
```

**After:**
```
pub fn handle_type_name(cpp_name: &str) -> String {
    // Underscore-preserving handle names (e.g. Handle_Geom_Curve,
    // Handle_NCollection_HArray1_gp_Pnt) — this mirrors OCCT's own classic
    // `Handle_X` typedef spelling and keeps the underlying class name readable.
    format!("Handle_{}", cpp_name.replace("::", "_"))
}

/// The small, fixed set of OCCT classes whose classic `Handle_X` typedef is
/// `Standard_DEPRECATED`. These are the classes declared with
/// `DEFINE_STANDARD_HANDLE` (which emits a deprecated `Handle_X` typedef) rather
/// than the usual `DEFINE_STANDARD_RTTIEXT` (which emits no `Handle_X` at all, so
/// the generator's own typedef is the sole, non-deprecated definition).
///
/// As of OCCT 8.0 these are the only three such classes in the public headers
/// (`grep -rl DEFINE_STANDARD_HANDLE target/OCCT/include/*.hxx`, excluding the
/// macro definition in `Standard_Handle.hxx`). If a future OCCT bump adds more,
/// they will resume warning until added here; re-run that grep to refresh.
pub fn handle_typedef_is_deprecated(cpp_name: &str) -> bool {
    matches!(
        cpp_name,
        "Standard_Type" | "math_NotSquare" | "math_SingularMatrix"
    )
}

/// C++ type spelling for a handle to `cpp_name`, for use in generated wrapper
/// bodies. Normally this is the classic `Handle_X` typedef (readable, and defined
/// by the generator for every handle-able class). For the few classes whose
/// `Handle_X` typedef OCCT marks `Standard_DEPRECATED` (see
/// [`handle_typedef_is_deprecated`]), it returns the canonical
/// `opencascade::handle<X>` form instead — an ABI-identical spelling that avoids
/// `-Wdeprecated-declarations`. FFI *symbol* names keep using [`handle_type_name`].
pub fn handle_cpp_type(cpp_name: &str) -> String {
    if handle_typedef_is_deprecated(cpp_name) {
        format!("opencascade::handle<{}>", cpp_name)
    } else {
        handle_type_name(cpp_name)
    }
}
```
</details>

    // 9. Handle upcast wrappers. The handle names in the struct are FFI symbol
    // spellings; for the C++ *types* use the canonical handle spelling (which only
    // differs from Handle_X for classes whose typedef OCCT deprecates). The derived
    // side is the class being emitted (`ffi_cn`); the base side is `hup.base_class`.
    for hup in &bindings.handle_upcasts {
        let base_handle = type_mapping::handle_cpp_type(&hup.base_class);
        let derived_handle = type_mapping::handle_cpp_type(ffi_cn);
        writeln!(
            output,
            "extern \"C\" OcctResult<{base_handle}*> {fn_name}(const {derived_handle}* self_) {{",
            fn_name = hup.ffi_fn_name,
        )
        .unwrap();
        writeln!(output, "    try {{ return {{new {base_handle}(*self_), nullptr}}; }}").unwrap();
        writeln!(output, "    OCCT_CATCH_RETURN").unwrap();
        writeln!(output, "}}").unwrap();
    }
```

    // 9b. Handle downcast wrappers. As with upcasts, use canonical handle spellings
    // for the C++ types. The base side is the class being emitted (`ffi_cn`); the
    // derived side is `hdown.derived_class`.
    for hdown in &bindings.handle_downcasts {
        let derived_handle = type_mapping::handle_cpp_type(&hdown.derived_class);
        let base_handle = type_mapping::handle_cpp_type(ffi_cn);
        writeln!(
            output,
            "extern \"C\" OcctResult<{derived_handle}*> {fn_name}(const {base_handle}* self_) {{",
            fn_name = hdown.ffi_fn_name,
        )
        .unwrap();
        writeln!(output, "    try {{").unwrap();
        writeln!(
            output,
            "    opencascade::handle<{derived_class}> result = opencascade::handle<{derived_class}>::DownCast(*self_);",
            derived_class = hdown.derived_class
        )
        .unwrap();
        writeln!(output, "    if (result.IsNull()) {{ return {{nullptr, nullptr}}; }}").unwrap();
        writeln!(
            output,
            "    return {{new {derived_handle}(result), nullptr}};",
        )
        .unwrap();

---
name: deprecation-warnings-canonicalized
description: How cargo build's C++ -Wdeprecated-declarations warnings were cut from ~1082 to 30 without changing FFI surface
metadata:
  type: project
---

`cargo build` emitted ~1082 C++ `-Wdeprecated-declarations` warnings from generated
wrappers. Fixed (2026-07-23) by canonicalizing deprecated OCCT type *spellings* in the
emitted C++ while keeping FFI *symbol names* unchanged → **1082 → 30**, zero surface
change (identical extern-C symbol set, zero generated `.rs` diff).

Mechanism:
- `codegen/collections.rs`: templates now emit `int` (not `Standard_Integer`) for
  index/length params. `canonical_cpp_primitive()` (bare type, e.g. `Standard_Boolean`→
  `bool`) + `canonicalize_cpp_spelling()` (regex whole-word, for `NCollection_HArray1<
  Standard_Boolean>`→`<bool>`) applied to element/value/cpp_spelling. Symbol names like
  `NCollection_HArray1_Standard_Boolean` are kept (the `_`-joined alias isn't a `\b` token).
- `type_mapping.rs`: `handle_cpp_type()` emits canonical `opencascade::handle<X>` in C++
  type positions for the classes whose `Handle_X` typedef OCCT marks deprecated, keeping
  `Handle_X` only as the FFI symbol prefix. Only **3** OCCT classes use the deprecating
  `DEFINE_STANDARD_HANDLE` macro (vs the usual `DEFINE_STANDARD_RTTIEXT`, which defines no
  `Handle_X`): `Standard_Type`, `math_NotSquare`, `math_SingularMatrix` — see
  `handle_typedef_is_deprecated()`. A redeclared typedef does NOT clear clang's deprecation,
  so the canonical spelling is the fix. Handle emission lives in TWO loops in `codegen/cpp.rs`
  (~line 767 and ~995) plus to_handle/get/upcast/downcast in `codegen/bindings.rs` — all patched.
- Preamble template-instantiation typedefs (`codegen/cpp.rs` `generate_preamble` + the
  in-file variant) also canonicalized.

**Irreducible remainder (30 warnings): `Standard_Mutex` (17) + `Transfer_TransferDeadLoop`
(13).** Both are fully-bound deprecated OCCT *classes* (auto-resolved deps, not in
bindings.toml), so there's no non-deprecated OCCT symbol to swap to: Standard_Mutex's
replacement is `std::mutex` (a non-OCCT stdlib type, different API); Transfer_TransferDeadLoop
is a dead exception with no replacement. Silencing them needs either exclusion (reduces
surface) or a scoped `#pragma`, not a symbol swap — left for the user to decide.

Related: [[collections-deprecated-aliases]], [[occt-8-binding-breakages-fixed]].

```

## Result: 1079 → 30 deprecation warnings, zero binding-surface change

`cargo build`'s C++ `-Wdeprecated-declarations` warnings came entirely from generated wrappers spelling types with **deprecated OCCT aliases**. Where a non-deprecated, ABI-identical spelling exists, I swapped it in the emitted C++ while keeping every FFI **symbol name** unchanged.

## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdZUnZVJwmXQSQDsb5eWj -->

No — and the evidence is unusually clear-cut on both halves of your question.

## Nothing requires them

Neither type appears in **any** other public OCCT header — not as a parameter, return type, base class, or member. There isn't even an `#include` of `Standard_Mutex.hxx` or `Transfer_TransferDeadLoop.hxx` anywhere in the installed headers. They're isolated leaves, so no other binding can reach them and excluding them can't cut off access to anything else.

## OCCT itself doesn't use them

- **`Transfer_TransferDeadLoop`**: **zero** references in the entire OCCT source tree outside its own `.hxx`/`.cxx` and a `FILES.cmake` listing. It is never thrown, exactly as its deprecation message says. A client cannot catch what is never raised. Bound surface: 3 wrappers (ctor, destructor, `exception_type`).
- **`Standard_Mutex`**: only 3 files mention it — its own `.hxx`, `.cxx`, and `FILES.cmake`. OCCT has fully migrated off it internally. Bound surface: 5 wrappers (ctor/lock/try_lock/unlock/destructor) plus `Sentry`.

## Would a client want them directly?

For `Standard_Mutex`, no — and this is the case where "no non-deprecated alternative" is misleading. There's no OCCT *capability* gated behind it; it's just a mutex. The replacement is `std::mutex`, and a Rust client should reach for `std::sync::Mutex`, which is strictly safer than driving a C++ mutex across FFI: the guard is tied to the data, and there's no manual `lock`/`unlock` pair that can be skipped on an early return or unwind. Exposing raw lock/unlock over FFI is arguably a footgun rather than a feature.

For `Transfer_TransferDeadLoop`, also no — a dead exception type.

## A relevant precedent in your own generator

The parser **already skips deprecated methods and constructors** ([parser.rs:705](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/parser.rs:705), [:863](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/parser.rs:863), [:906](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/parser.rs:906)) via `Availability::Deprecated`. So "don't bind deprecated things" is established policy — it just isn't applied at *class* granularity. Both of these are class-level `Standard_DEPRECATED`, which clang reports the same way.

Blast radius is tiny: only 5 headers in all of OCCT have a class-level `Standard_DEPRECATED`, and of those you bind exactly these 2. `BRepMesh_FastDiscret` and `Message_ProgressSentry` aren't bound at all, and the deprecated `TCollection` class isn't used either (the 282 `TCollection_*` symbols are `TCollection_AsciiString`/`ExtendedString` — different classes, and they produce no warnings).

## Recommendation

Given your goal is a *more complete* FFI layer, I'd go with the **scoped `#pragma clang diagnostic push/ignored("-Wdeprecated-declarations")/pop`** around those two wrapper blocks: it reaches 0 warnings while keeping every symbol, and it's future-proof for OCCT's ongoing deprecation campaign — when they deprecate something genuinely useful, you'll want the binding to survive without noise.

Extending the existing deprecation skip to classes is the tidier alternative and matches your current policy, but it does trade away 8 wrappers. Since those 8 are provably dead, that trade is defensible — it just isn't the "keep the surface" answer you set out to get.

Want me to implement either one? Both are small generator changes.

716	/// Parse a class or struct declaration.
717	/// Returns a vector because nested classes/structs defined inside the class
718	/// are also returned (qualified as \`Parent::Nested\`).
719	fn parse_class(entity: &Entity, source_header: &str, verbose: bool) -> Vec<ParsedClass> {
720	    let name = match entity.get_name() {
721	        Some(n) => n,
722	        None => return Vec::new(),
723	    };
724	
725	    // Skip forward declarations (no definition)
726	    if !entity.is_definition() {
727	        return Vec::new();
728	    }
729	
730	    // Skip anonymous classes/structs
731	    if name.is_empty() {
732	        return Vec::new();
733	    }
734	
735	    // Skip internal/private classes (those starting with underscore)
736	    if name.starts_with('_') {
737	        return Vec::new();
738	    }
739	
740	    // Skip template classes and template specializations
741	    // Template classes have get_template() returning Some, or get_template_kind() returning Some
742	    // Also skip if the display name contains angle brackets (indicates template instantiation)
743	    if entity.get_template().is_some() {
744	        if verbose {
745	            println!("    Skipping {} (template class)", name);
746	        }
747	        return Vec::new();
748	    }
749	    let display_name = entity.get_display_name().unwrap_or_default();
750	    if display_name.contains('<') {
751	        if verbose {
752	            println!("    Skipping {} (template specialization)", display_name);
753	        }
754	        return Vec::new();
755	    }
756	
757	    // Skip policy/trait classes used as template parameters
758	    // These are not meant to be instantiated directly
759	    if name.contains("Inspector") || name.contains("_Hasher") || name.contains("_Traits") {
760	        if verbose {
761	            println!("    Skipping {} (policy/trait class)", name);
762	        }
763	        return Vec::new();
764	    }
765	
766	    // Skip internal node types that use custom allocators (can't be used with std::unique_ptr)
767	    if name.ends_with("Node") && name.starts_with("NCollection_") {
768	        if verbose {
769	            println!("    Skipping {} (internal node type)", name);
770	        }
771	        return Vec::new();
772	    }
773	
774	    let comment = extract_doxygen_comment(entity);
775	    let module = extract_module_from_header(source_header);
776	
777	    // Extract direct base classes for upcast generation
778	    let base_classes = extract_base_classes(entity);
779	    
780	    // Check for protected/private destructor (indicates non-instantiable abstract base class)
781	    let has_protected_destructor = check_protected_destructor(entity);
782	
783	    if verbose {
784	        println!("  Parsing class: {}", name);
785	        if !base_classes.is_empty() {
786	            println!("    Base classes: {:?}", base_classes);
787	        }
788	        if has_protected_destructor {
789	            println!("    Has protected destructor (non-instantiable)");
790	        }
791	    }
792	
793	    let mut constructors = Vec::new();
794	    let mut methods = Vec::new();
795	    let mut static_methods = Vec::new();

    // Skip internal/private classes (those starting with underscore)
    if name.starts_with('_') {
        return Vec::new();
    }

    // Skip classes marked Standard_DEPRECATED, matching the existing policy for
    // deprecated functions/constructors/methods. Returning early also drops any
    // nested classes (e.g. Standard_Mutex::Sentry), which are deprecated with
    // their parent. Binding these emits -Wdeprecated-declarations warnings for
    // types OCCT no longer uses itself and that no other header references.
    if entity.get_availability() == Availability::Deprecated {
        if verbose {
            println!("    Skipping {} (deprecated class)", name);
        }
        return Vec::new();
    }
```

=== Existing commit message (HEAD) ===
Canonicalize deprecated OCCT type spellings in generated C++ (1082 -> 30 warnings)

The generated C++ wrappers referenced OCCT scalar aliases (Standard_Integer,
Standard_Boolean, ...) and the classic Handle_X typedefs, which OCCT 8.0 marks
\`Standard_DEPRECATED\`, producing ~1082 -Wdeprecated-declarations warnings during
\`cargo build\`.

Emit the canonical C++ spelling in wrapper bodies instead — \`int\` for
\`Standard_Integer\`, \`opencascade::handle<X>\` for the deprecated \`Handle_X\`
typedefs (only \`Standard_Type\`, \`math_NotSquare\`, \`math_SingularMatrix\` use the
deprecating \`DEFINE_STANDARD_HANDLE\` macro). The extern-C symbol names are
unchanged (\`handle_type_name\`/\`typedef_name\`/overload tokens keep the original
spelling), so the FFI surface is byte-identical (zero generated .rs diff) and the
substitutions are ABI-identical typedef swaps.

Cuts the warnings 1082 -> 30; the remaining 30 (Standard_Mutex, and
Transfer_TransferDeadLoop) are fully-bound deprecated OCCT *classes* with no
non-deprecated symbol to swap to.

Eliminate -Wdeprecated-declarations warnings in generated C++ (1079 -> 0)

C++ wrappers. Two independent causes, fixed separately.

1. Deprecated type *spellings* (1079 -> 30), no FFI surface change

The wrappers spelled types using OCCT scalar aliases (Standard_Integer,
Standard_Boolean) and the classic Handle_X typedefs, all of which OCCT 8.0 marks
`Standard_DEPRECATED`. Emit the canonical spelling in wrapper bodies instead:

  - `int` / `bool` for the scalar aliases. collections.rs gains
    `canonical_cpp_primitive` (bare type) and `canonicalize_cpp_spelling`
    (whole-word regex, for template spellings like
    `NCollection_HArray1<Standard_Boolean>` -> `<bool>`).
  - `opencascade::handle<X>` for the deprecated `Handle_X` typedefs, via
    `type_mapping::handle_cpp_type`. Only three OCCT classes declare their handle
    with the deprecating `DEFINE_STANDARD_HANDLE` macro (Standard_Type,
    math_NotSquare, math_SingularMatrix); everything else uses
    DEFINE_STANDARD_RTTIEXT, which defines no Handle_X at all, so the generator's
    own typedef is the sole, non-deprecated definition. Redeclaring the typedef
    does not clear clang's deprecation attribute, so the canonical spelling is
    the only fix. Handle wrappers are emitted from two loops in codegen/cpp.rs
    plus to_handle/get/upcast/downcast in codegen/bindings.rs; all are covered.

extern-C symbol names are derived separately (handle_type_name, typedef_name,
overload suffix tokens) and keep the original spelling, so this part is a pure
spelling change: the exported symbol set is identical and the generated .rs files
have a zero diff. Every substitution is an exact typedef, hence ABI-identical.

2. Deprecated *classes* (30 -> 0), small provably-dead surface removal

The remaining 30 warnings came from Standard_Mutex and Transfer_TransferDeadLoop,
which are deprecated OCCT classes rather than aliases — there is no
non-deprecated symbol to swap to. The parser already skips deprecated functions,
constructors and methods via `Availability::Deprecated`; apply the same rule at
class granularity in `parse_class`. Returning early also drops nested classes
(Standard_Mutex::Sentry), which are deprecated along with their parent.

This drops exactly 23 extern-C symbols (12 Standard_Mutex incl. Sentry, 11
Transfer_TransferDeadLoop) and adds none. The removed surface is dead:

  - Neither type is referenced by any other installed OCCT header — not as a
    parameter, return type, base class or member — and no header #includes
    either one. They are isolated leaves, so nothing else can need them.
  - Transfer_TransferDeadLoop has zero references anywhere in the OCCT source
    tree outside its own files; it is never thrown, matching its deprecation
    note. It cannot be caught, so binding it buys nothing.
  - Standard_Mutex is mentioned only by its own .hxx/.cxx; OCCT no longer uses it
    internally. Its documented replacement is std::mutex, and Rust clients have
    std::sync::Mutex, which is safer than driving a C++ mutex over FFI.

The parent classes stay bound, so exception coverage is unaffected:
Standard_Failure, Transfer_TransferFailure (the exception actually thrown),
Interface_InterfaceError and Standard_ErrorHandler all remain. No dangling
references to the removed types remain in the generated output.

Only 5 installed headers carry a class-level Standard_DEPRECATED; of those just
these 2 were bound (BRepMesh_FastDiscret and Message_ProgressSentry are not
bound, and the deprecated TCollection class is unused), so the new rule has no
other effect today.

Verification: `cargo build` reports 0 deprecation warnings (the 3 remaining
-Wdelete-incomplete warnings are pre-existing and unrelated); 22 generator tests
and the full workspace test suite pass.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>

```

## 🤖 Assistant
<!-- msg_011CdZVXDM3y45nLQeEh2jZj -->

Done. **Deprecation warnings are now at zero**, and the commit is amended.

## The change

Extended the parser's existing deprecation skip to class granularity in [parser.rs](crates/opencascade-binding-generator/src/parser.rs) — `parse_class` now returns early on `Availability::Deprecated`, exactly as it already did for functions, constructors, and methods. The early return also drops nested classes, which handles `Standard_Mutex::Sentry` for free.

## Impact — measured, not assumed

- **23 extern-C symbols removed, 0 added** — 12 from `Standard_Mutex` (incl. `Sentry`), 11 from `Transfer_TransferDeadLoop`. No collateral loss anywhere else.
- **Parent classes intact**: `Standard_Failure`, `Transfer_TransferFailure` (the exception that *is* actually thrown), `Interface_InterfaceError`, `Standard_ErrorHandler` all still bound. Zero dangling references to the removed types.
- Only 5 installed headers carry a class-level `Standard_DEPRECATED`; just these 2 were bound, so the new rule has no other effect today.

## Warnings, precisely

The original 1082 figure in the old commit message was slightly off — it was clang's total, comprising **1079 `-Wdeprecated-declarations` + 3 `-Wdelete-incomplete`**. So the accurate result is **1079 → 0** deprecation warnings. The 3 `-Wdelete-incomplete` remain; they're pre-existing and unrelated, and I said so in the commit message rather than claiming a clean zero.

## Verification

`scripts/regenerate-bindings.sh` → `cargo build` (0 deprecation warnings) → 22 generator tests pass → full workspace suite passes, 0 failures.

## Commit

Amended to `d8c58a2`, working tree clean. The message now covers both halves as one coherent story: part 1 (spelling canonicalization, zero surface change) and part 2 (deprecated-class skip), including the evidence that the removed surface is dead — no other header references or includes either type, `Transfer_TransferDeadLoop` has zero references in all of OCCT's source, and `Standard_Mutex` is mentioned only by its own files.

One forward-looking note I put in the commit and memory: this rule is right *because* these two were dead. If OCCT later deprecates a class that's genuinely useful, the better move is a scoped `#pragma clang diagnostic ignored` around its wrappers rather than silently losing the binding — otherwise the skip will quietly erode surface as OCCT's deprecation campaign continues.
